present on `main` and in released providers)\\r\\n\\r\\n### What happened?\\r\\n\\r\\nThe OpenLineage listener submits **bound adapter methods** to its DAG-run event `ProcessPoolExecutor`:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `self.adapter.dag_started` / `dag_success` / `dag_failed` in the DAG-run hooks: [listener.py#L981-L982](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/[REDACTED]/providers/openlineage/src/airflow/providers/openlineage/plugins/listener.py#L981-L982), [#L1033-L1034](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/[REDACTED]/providers/openlineage/src/airflow/providers/openlineage/plugins/listener.py#L1033-L1034), [#L1084-L1085](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/[REDACTED]/providers/openlineage/src/airflow/providers/openlineage/plugins/listener.py#L1084-L1085)\\r\\n- `self.adapter.fail_task` / `complete_task` passed as an argument to `_emit_manual_state_change_event`: [listener.py#L868-L873](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/[REDACTED]/providers/openlineage/src/airflow/providers/openlineage/plugins/listener.py#L868-L873)\\r\\n\\r\\nPickling a bound method serializes the adapter itself, so the long-lived pool worker unpickles a **fresh `OpenLineageAdapter` for every event** and, on emit, builds a **new `OpenLineageClient` — with a new transport set — per DAG-run state change** ([`get_or_create_openlineage_client` caches on `self._client`, an instance attribute](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/[REDACTED]/providers/openlineage/src/airflow/providers/openlineage/plugins/adapter.py#L87-L107)). `close()` is never called on those clients.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor transports that start background worker threads this leaks one thread per event inside the scheduler. The `datadog` transport (openlineage-python \\u003e= 1.37.0) unconditionally starts an `AsyncHttpTransport` worker thread in its constructor, and each such thread busy-polls its queue (`await asyncio.sleep(0.01)` when idle). Measured on openlineage-python 1.47.1, each leaked idle thread costs ~0.45% CPU:\\r\\n\\r\\n| clients created (= events) | threads | idle CPU% |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 0 | 1 | 0.0 |\\r\\n| 50 | 51 | 29.5 |\\r\\n| 150 | 151 | 72.0 |\\r\\n| 400 | 401 | 167.6 |\\r\\n\\r\\nProduction impact (Airflow 2.11.2, Astronomer, KubernetesExecutor, ~50 hourly DAGs, composite transport with a `datadog` leg for Datadog Jobs Monitoring): scheduler CPU climbs steadily (non-sawtooth) from the moment the datadog leg is enabled until pinned at its limit within ~6 hours; scheduler heartbeat dips; liveness-probe restarts temporarily recover it. Disabling OpenLineage restores normal behavior immediately. Routing all events through the transport's async queue (`ASYNC_TRANSPORT_RULES={\\\"*\\\": {\\\"*\\\": true}}`) does not help, because the worker thread is created in the transport constructor regardless of routing.\\r\\n\\r\\n### What you think should happen instead?\\r\\n\\r\\nEach pool worker should keep exactly one `OpenLineageClient` (one transport set, one set of transport threads) for its whole lifetime, regardless of how many events it emits.\\r\\n\\r\\n### How to reproduce\\r\\n\\r\\nOne `DatadogTransport` instantiation is what each per-event client rebuild does in the pool worker:\\r\\n\\r\\n```python\\r\\nimport threading\\r\\nimport time\\r\\n\\r\\nfrom openlineage.client.tra
2026-10-30T02:56:08.8118298Z ...
